## The headline for step 9's size

The ctor doors do not die by converting callers: of the ~117 polymorphic Runtime
classes that still have a registered ctor door, only 18 have a real C++ ctor sharing the door's body (the B.3.8
RB bases); 67 declare a protected `X() = default` placeholder (the B34 §4.3 "not constructible" guard) and 25 declare
no ctor at all (header scan, T1 column "C++ today"). Every one of those ~92 classes is built ONLY by its C-style door
(base door by address or by name + a stamp of the minted table + field stores), reached from `Object::Factory`
create / create-in-place, from `Description::CreateInstance`-style members, and from derived doors. The mint rows
of those classes retire only after each class gets a real ctor (the door body moved into it, B8-1), its factory
entries become `new (p) X()`, and its `CreateInstance` / `Construct` / `Reconstruct` members become `::new (p) X()`.
That is Runtime-side class work of the size of step 6 (UI) and step 5 (Video/Graphics), not a caller sweep.

## Mechanism (question B, precisely)

1. **The row.** `RT_DELDTOR(ADDR, DTOR, SIZE, NAME)` (deleting_dtor.cpp:285) is an aggregate initialiser
   `{ddVa, dtorVa, elemSize, name}` in `rtdtor::kSpecs[]` (deleting_dtor.cpp:287-1184); `kSpecCount = 135`
   (deleting_dtor_internal.h:66, `static_assert`ed at deleting_dtor.cpp:1187). The "137" of NEXT_SESSION §3a is a
   grep count: 135 rows + the `#define` (:285) + the comment at :278.
2. **The body.** One entry point per row, `rtdtor::RtDelDtor_<Class>(void* self, unsigned flags)` =
   `DeletingDtorCore(self, flags, i)` (deleting_dtor.cpp:1263-1817), i.e. MSVC's VECTOR deleting destructor:
   `flags & 2` → `EhVectorDtor` over the cookie at `this-8` (:1214) and free `this-8`; else `~Class(this)` and
   `rtcoll::MemFree(this)` on `flags & 1` (:1238-1260). `~Class` is `Dtor(i)` (:1199): a LAZY, per-row cached
   `ResolveAddr(Module::Runtime, dtorVa)` into `g_dtorCache[]` — first use fills it, from whatever thread
   dispatches first (a B6-5 first-use fill). `ResolveAddr` consults the port registry first, so `~Class` is the
   registered `??1` DOOR of that class (T1): **a `??1` door cannot be deleted while its row exists**, and two rows
   (EdgeMeshInstance, CompressedCopyFileStream) resolve to NULL today because nothing registers their `~Class`.
3. **The registration.** `RtObjectDeletingDtorPortInit()` (deleting_dtor.cpp:1844-2011) does
   `RegisterRuntimeAddr(ddVa, &RtDelDtor_X)` for all 135, by literal address (the VAs are unexported, so the
   registry's name mirror, resolve_standalone.cpp:251-260, adds no name). It runs from `RuntimePortInit`
   (resolve_standalone.cpp:346) AFTER every namespace's `*PortInit` (:281-338) and it also calls
   `RtObjectDeletingDtorLeavesRegister()` (:1946), so its registrations WIN over any earlier one of the same key
   (last write wins, resolve_standalone.cpp:239-260) — see finding 1.
4. **How a minted slot 0 finds its row.** Each minted table's slot 0 is an `RT_MINT_UNNAMED` entry carrying the
   DLL VA of the class's vector deleting dtor (e.g. `rt_data_mint_tables.inc:2340` `{ nullptr, 0x180035c10ull,
   RT_MINT_UNNAMED }` for `??_7Element@UI@@6B@`). The mint runs after port init (resolve_standalone.cpp:388) and
   `MintVtables()` (rt_data_mint.cpp:263) fills every non-purecall slot with `OurPort(s)` =
   `RtPortLookupAddr(dllVa)` (rt_data_mint.cpp:201-207), else a named `RT_MINT_TRAP`. So slot 0 of the minted table
   IS `RtDelDtor_X`. Measured: each of the 135 row VAs is slot 0 of exactly ONE minted table and appears in no other
   slot of any table (T4 `fills`); no row fills nothing.
5. **Who dispatches it.** Any slot-0 call on an object whose vptr is the minted table: `Object::Destroy(p)` /
   `p->DeletingDtor(1)` (virtual through the object's own vptr), `CallSlot0`, the raw `vt[0](p, f)` sites (T6), the
   Runtime's `InternalFactory` / `ExternalFactory` release paths. (ii) By-address calls of a row VA: **none** — the
   only non-mint occurrences of the 135 VAs are `static_assert` / comment strings in `runtime/types` headers, plus one
   coincidental dx11-image VA (devicedata.cpp:269, `0x1800134b0` is VideoDX11's import slot, not the Runtime's
   EdgeMesh dtor). (iii) Hand-built tables: **none** — no `RtDelDtor_` symbol is referenced outside deleting_dtor.cpp.
   So a row is reachable iff an object wearing its minted table can still exist, i.e. iff its table still has a
   stamper (T4 `stampers`, which is 0A's census from the other side). Every row's table has at least one.
6. **Supersession.** A compiler `DeletingDtor` supersedes a row only for objects built by a real C++ ctor (they
   wear the compiler table). It does nothing for door-built objects, which wear the minted table and reach the row.
   So "superseded" rows (T4) still retire WITH their mint row, never before: deleting such a row today turns slot 0 of
   a live minted table into a trap (B34_RULES §4.2, B33 §6.1).


## Findings (prose; each carries file:line)

1. **`??1SunLight` is registered twice and the older transcription wins.** RB's B8-1 door `Graphics_SunLight_Dtor`
   (sunlight.cpp:438, one body with `~SunLight` via `DoorBody::Release`) registers at sunlight.cpp:518-519 from
   `RtGraphicsPortInit` (resolve_standalone.cpp:298); `deleting_dtor_leaves.cpp`'s `RtGraphics_SunLight_Dtor` (:123,
   a C transcription that stamps the minted table and frees +0xf0/+0xf8/+0x100 by hand) registers the same name and
   VA at :206-207 from `RtObjectDeletingDtorPortInit` (resolve_standalone.cpp:346), later, so it overwrites both keys
   (resolve_standalone.cpp:239-260). The `RT_DELDTOR` row for SunLight (deleting_dtor.cpp:782) and every by-name
   caller therefore run the leaves body, not RB's. No other name in T1 has two door symbols.
2. **Two rows call a NULL `~Class`.** `Graphics::EdgeMeshInstance` (deleting_dtor.cpp:707, `~` = 0x1800174d0) and
   `IO::CompressedCopyFileStream` (:915, `~` = 0x1800a92a0): neither VA nor `??1` name is registered anywhere (the
   comment at :1552 says "PORTED"; compressedcopyfilestream.cpp:154-168 registers only Write/Read/Close).
   `Dtor(i)` returns 0 and `DeletingDtorCore` calls it. Reached only if a minted-table object of either class is
   destroyed through slot 0; not measured whether that happens (EdgeMeshInstance is embedded at `self+0x28` in the
   tool highlight, tool_base_render.cpp:512, and torn down by member calls, :583/:650).
3. **`deleting_dtor_leaves.cpp` holds 0 `RT_DELDTOR` rows and 11 dtor doors** (not "1 row + 5"): ToolTipDialog,
   ReaderWriter, Light, SunLight, AnimationGroup, SoundEffect, AchievementInstance, MaterialInstance, Base, Stream,
   ParticleInstance (:107-187, registered :189-212). Each door's ONLY caller is its class's `RT_DELDTOR` row (T2).
   Eight of the eleven classes now have a real C++ dtor that the door duplicates by hand instead of delegating to:
   `~AchievementInstance` (achievement_manager.cpp:260), `~MaterialInstance` (renderer.cpp:665),
   `~ParticleInstance` (ParticleInstance.h:165), `~SunLight` (SunLight.h:192), `~SoundEffect`
   (vtbatch7_soundbuffer_soundeffect.cpp:502), `~AnimationGroup` (vtbatch7_animgroup_particlesystem.cpp:499),
   `~ToolTipDialog` (tooltipdialog.cpp:419), `~Light` (lights_vtable.cpp:589). They die with their rows.
4. **The row → `??1` door coupling.** Because `Dtor(i)` looks `~Class` up by VA (deleting_dtor.cpp:1199-1204), the
   133 registered `??1` doors that a row names cannot go before the row: for 114 of the 143 polymorphic dtor doors
   the row is the ONLY caller (T1 verdict "dies with the mint", T2 group RTDELDTOR). Order inside step 9: row first, then door.
5. **The 13 superseding overrides are SCALAR, the rows are VECTOR.** Only `Internal::DeletingDtor`
   (internal_class.cpp:415) writes the `flags & 2` arm; Base (type_reflection.cpp:125), External
   (external_class.cpp:176), ExternalList (external_class_doors.cpp:348) and the nine inline ones (T4) do not.
   Equivalent while nothing `new[]`s these (B8-2), but the cutover to `virtual ~T()` changes the arm anyway.
   `BinaryFileStream::DeletingDtor` (file_stream.cpp:1314) and `MemoryStream::DeletingDtor` (memory_stream.cpp:233)
   are concrete but DELEGATE to their direct-registered doors (T5), so they supersede nothing.
6. **Five `RT_DELDTOR` classes are not C++ classes at all**: `Object::ExtensionCompiler`, `Object::ExternalCompiler`,
   `IO::CompressedCopyFileStream` (no class anywhere), `Collision2D::CollisionMesh` / `Surface` (file-local PODs,
   collisionmesh.cpp:105, surface.cpp:48). Two more have a class but no own slot 0: `Object::CompiledExternal`
   (External.h:692, public `= default` ctor, inherits `External::DeletingDtor` which runs `~External` and would SKIP
   `~CompiledExternal`'s restamp if a C++-built one were destroyed — behaviour-neutral today, nothing builds one) and
   `Graphics::Light` (Light.h:110, abstract).
7. **Real destructors still stamp the MINT.** The twelve `Video::XData::~XData()` bodies hand-store the minted
   `Vt_X()` table (companion_data.cpp:273-276, "THE STORE IS WRITTEN OUT BY HAND") and the twelve `*Data` ctor doors
   stamp it too (companion_data.cpp:158-165); dx11 builds every companion object through those doors
   (devicedata.cpp:2030-2111 `AllocData`, exports.cpp:100) and then stamps its own derived table. The minted
   `??_7XData` rows (and their 12 `RT_DELDTOR` rows) are reachable only mid-construction / mid-destruction, but they
   cannot retire before the carry-over-6 dx11 seam decision.
8. **Exe-side door-then-stamp construction survives step 8.** Besides the 18 loop builders NEXT_SESSION §3a names
   (T2: 14 door calls — `fn_UpdateLoop_Ctor` 3, `fn_LoaderLoop_Ctor` 5, `fn_DialogLoaderLoopT_Ctor` 5,
   `fn_DialogLoopT_Ctor` 1 in engine_funcs.h:2203 — plus 3 readiness checks, tutorial_next_scenario.cpp:147-151): `PathfinderThread` is built by the `??0Thread` door then
   `PathfinderThread::Vtable()` stamped (pathfinder_seed.cpp:148-151); Works/Actions are built by the `??0Internal`
   door through exe slot 0x140152768 and then stamped (work_base_reimpl.cpp:159, clearareacomponent_states.cpp:337,
   citizenai_actionctors.cpp:191 and :893, citizenai_handlers.cpp:152); `MeshInstance` by exe slots
   0x140151490/0x1401514a0/0x1401514a8 in 7 files (T2); thumbnails by `fn_MaterialInstance_Ctor` /
   `fn_SpriteSheet_Ctor` / `fn_ImageBuffer_Ctor` (mapui.cpp:76/118, moddialog.cpp:400/416/454, noiseui.cpp:83/121/298,
   saveloaddialog.cpp:248/292, minimap_paint.cpp:150, minimap_upload.cpp:63, serialize_image_upload.cpp:178).
9. **The mint itself calls doors.** `rt_data_mint.cpp:460-461` constructs the `?_loader` storage with the
   `??0Thread@System@@QEAA@I@Z` door (looked up with `RtPortLookupExport`) and stamps the minted `??_7Loader`;
   :425 its teardown calls the `??1Thread` door. The singleton-storage retirement (D10's last item) owns these two.
10. **Table-driven doors a literal grep misses.** 14 UI ctor exports are registered only by `factory_ui1.cpp`'s
    `RegisterOne` (:310-322, `sp.ctorName` from `factory_ui1_tables.inc`, body `RunCtor` via `ThunkCtor<I>` :276/:303)
    and its base-ctor call resolves a string at run time (`BaseCtor`, :270-273, 15 `baseCtor` rows). Likewise dx11's
    import struct (devicedata.cpp:259-279) and `rt_factories_*.inc` `RtFactOp::Call` rows (26) call doors by name.
11. **Dead bindings (delete now, T2 group DEAD):** engine_funcs `fn_External_External` / `fn_External_Dtor`
    (engine_funcs.cpp:1315 / :1322, zero readers — so the exe slot rows address_registry.cpp:1317-1318 / :1323-1324
    have no reader either), `fn_DialogInstance_Dtor` (:921), `fn_Element_Ctor` (:2113), `fn_ElementDescription_Ctor`
    / `_Dtor` (:2111 / :2109; their exe slot rows address_registry.cpp:851-860 are reader-less too), `fn_Goal_BaseCtor`
    (:2276), `fn_Graphics_Geometry_Ctor` (:2421); five function-local statics resolved and never called
    (`s_wac_/s_wah_/s_wap2_/s_wap_/s_fn_ObjInternalCtor`, work_act_clear.cpp:130, work_act_hunt.cpp:121,
    work_act_path.cpp:145, work_act_perform.cpp:157, work_base_actionctor.cpp:154). **Dead but gating:**
    `fn_ObjectInternal_Dtor` and five `s_fn_ObjectInternal_Dtor` statics are never CALLED but are conjuncts of the
    `ok` readiness flags of ten Action `ResolveFuncs` (e.g. dropoffrawmaterial.cpp:100, huntanimal.cpp:144,
    completetask.cpp:93) — deleting them must drop the conjunct, not leave a null test.
12. **`Object::Destroy` is almost unused.** Outside `tests/` it has 2 call sites (achievement_state.cpp:156,
    batchc_goallist_dtor.cpp:67). Destruction goes through `->DeletingDtor(1)` (24), `CallSlot0` (40), raw
    `vt[0](p, f)` (64, some of them NOT deletes — see T6 note) and `DeletingDtor(0)` self-calls in
    `Destruct`/`Reconstruct` (289). B8-2's "`Object::Destroy(p)` replaces `CallSlot0(p, 1)`" was applied to code/Game
    only; every remaining `CallSlot0` is in `runtime/src` (T6).

## Part C — what the normal-virtual cutover touches (size only)

From T6 (all counts are code lines; comment lines excluded):
- **`DeletingDtor(unsigned)` declarations and definitions: 901 lines** (327 of them out-of-line `X::DeletingDtor`
  definitions): code/Game 612 (Component 314, Dialog 122, Work 46, Tutorial 42, Game 38, Action 36, Player 24,
  Goal 10, Entity 2), runtime/types 136 (ui 68, graphics 19, video 19, io 14, achievement/engine/object 4 each,
  audio 2, pathfinding/picking 1 each), runtime/src 6, standalone 4, tests 121 (the `*_abi_test` pins). Each becomes a
  `virtual ~T()` (declaration) or disappears (the out-of-line scalar bodies).
- **Self-calls `DeletingDtor(0)` (the `Destruct`/`Reconstruct` slot bodies): 289** — code/Game 231 (Component 166,
  Tutorial 27, Game 26, Goal 8, Dialog 4), runtime/src 58 (ui 56, achievement 2). Each becomes `this->~T()` (non-
  deleting), which after the cutover is a virtual-dtor call; B5-3's "never `DeletingDtor(0)` on the dx11 side" stands.
- **`p->DeletingDtor(1)`: 24** (runtime/src/video 10, ui 5, engine 2, graphics 2, code/Game/Game 2, standalone 1,
  runtime/types/object 1 = `Object::Destroy` itself, tests 1) → `delete p`.
- **`Object::Destroy(p)`: 19** (tests 17, runtime/src/achievement 2) → `delete p`.
- **`CallSlot0(p, f)`: 40 calls + 19 file-local definitions** (runtime/src/video 12, graphics 8, ui 8, audio 4,
  collision2d 4, pathfinding 2, picking 2) — all are `Destruct()` bodies calling slot 0 with flag 0 → `this->~T()`.
- **Raw slot-0 dispatch `vt[0](p, f)` / `(*(void***)p)[0](p, f)`: 64** (both spellings) (runtime/src/graphics 13, video 13, ui 8,
  collision2d 4, io 3, object 3, save 2, audio 2, pathfinding 2, picking 2, engine 1, types 1; code/Game/Dialog 4,
  Game 3, Player 2; tests 1). NOT all are deletes: graphicsmesh_vtable.cpp:611/:613 (`Encoding::IsBinary`),
  edgemesh_vtable.cpp:501 (encoding), thread_win.cpp:400 (`Thread::Run`), statemachine.cpp:55 (a state handler) read
  slot 0 of tables whose slot 0 is not a dtor (T5). The deleting ones become `delete p` / `p->~T()`.
- **Explicit `~T()` calls (`->~X()`, `X::~X()`): 824** (code/Game 631, runtime 156, tests 25, dx11 7, standalone 5)
  — unchanged by the cutover in spelling, but every one on a class whose dtor turns virtual must be re-read: a
  qualified `X::~X()` stays non-virtual (the companion_data.cpp:218 reason), an unqualified one starts dispatching.

## Part D — pure slot 0 classes (T7)

121 Runtime classes declare slot 0 `= 0` today (runtime/types: ui 65, video 18, graphics 15, io 12, engine 4,
achievement 3, audio 2, pathfinding 1, picking 1; exact rows in T7). 115 are `RT_DELDTOR` rows; 6 are direct-registered
deleting-dtor bodies (IO::FileStream, VisitorStream, BinaryReader, BinaryWriter, Visitor; UI::StringTable). None is
in code/Game or engine (the exe classes all have concrete `DeletingDtor` since step 8). Each needs, in step 9: a real
ctor (most have `X() = default` protected or none — T1), a protected non-virtual `~X()` with the door's body, a
concrete `DeletingDtor(unsigned)` (B8-2 shape), and only then may its door and row go. Plus the 7 row classes of
finding 6 that have no slot 0 declaration at all.

## Retirement consequences

**Deletable at once (no conversion, no behaviour change):** the dead bindings of finding 11 (8 engine_funcs globals
and their declarations in engine_funcs.h, 5 code/Game statics); the reader-less exe slot rows of `??0/??1External`
(address_registry.cpp:1317-1318, :1323-1324), `??0/??1ElementDescription` (:851-854) and `??0/??1Element`
(:857-860) once those globals go; the `??0Goal` door's only binding (`fn_Goal_BaseCtor`) — the door itself stays
until `Goal`'s mint row goes (it has no caller then). Nothing in the `RT_DELDTOR` table is deletable at once.

**Needs a conversion first (the step-9 work):**
- Per Runtime class (by namespace, the FILEMAP blocks): real ctor sharing the door body (B8-1), concrete
  `DeletingDtor` (T7), `Object::Factory` create/create-in-place = `new (p) X()` (the `ui_factory_wave28.cpp`
  thunks, `factory_ui1` table, `rt_factories_*.inc` replay rows), members that build an instance of another class
  (`Description::CreateInstance`, `Construct`/`Reconstruct`) = `::new (p) X()`, derived classes calling the base
  in the init list. Then: the mint row (0A), the `RT_DELDTOR` row (T4), and the ctor + dtor doors (T1) go together.
- Exe-side callers (T2 group EXT, finding 8): the 18 loop builders → real loop ctors; the thumbnail/minimap/menu
  `::new (p) MaterialInstance()` / `SpriteSheet()` / `ImageBuffer()` / `MeshInstance()` / `SunLight()` /
  `ParticleSystemInstance()`; `PathfinderThread` and the five `??0Internal`-then-stamp Work/Action builders →
  their real ctors; `w2_takescreenshot.cpp` FrameCopyImageBuffer and `gameupdateloop_serialize.cpp`
  CompressedMemoryStream (by-value locals or `::new`); `DialogInstance`'s 6-arg door (4 sites: edittool_select.cpp:228,
  mainmenudialog.cpp:495, toolbardialog.cpp:636) → `::new (p) UI::DialogInstance(ui, dialog, …)` (the real ctor
  exists, DialogInstance.h:528).
- Fix before deleting anything that relies on it: finding 1 (decide which `??1SunLight` body is THE body — RB's —
  and drop the leaves registration), finding 2 (register or port the two NULL `~Class`), finding 5 (BinaryFileStream
  and MemoryStream overrides must own a body before their direct doors go).

**Must stay (and why):**
- The 12 `Video::*Data` ctor doors (dx11 calls them through its import table) and the 12 `*Data` rows: the dx11
  `*Data` seam is carry-over 6 (B35_RULES §11, RULE B5-8); retiring them is that decision, not step 9's mint work.
- The `??0/??1Thread` doors while the mint constructs `?_loader` (finding 9) — until the singleton-storage step.
- Every non-polymorphic door (T3, 102 with callers): no vtable, no mint row; they belong to the registry teardown
  after B.3, together with the B4b-2 by-value holders (`System::Time` 181 bound sites, `Random`, `Filename`, `String`).
- `deleting_dtor_internal.h`'s `rtcoll::MemFree` / `MemAlloc` declarations outlive the table: they are included by
  class_operator_delete.cpp:51, ui_operator_delete.cpp:55 and vtbatch7_animgroup_particlesystem.cpp:168 — emptying
  `kSpecs` must not delete the header.

## Not measured

- Whether any slot-0 dispatch reaches the two NULL-`~Class` rows at run time (finding 2), or any `*Data` row
  (finding 7): needs a run; nothing was run.
- The `stamp` column reads the door's own body and one level of same-file helper; a stamp two calls deep, or in
  another file, reads "no vptr store in body". The authoritative stamper census per table is 0A's (INVENTORY_A).
- Caller attribution through a local variable is windowed to the variable's function (40 lines, stops at the next
  assignment); a door pointer copied through a struct field (dx11's `g_im` / `im->`) was followed by hand for
  `CtorDeviceData` (exports.cpp:100) and the `AllocData` sites, not generically.
- "C++ today" is a header scan of `runtime/types` + `runtime/src` headers (class body attribution by brace depth);
  classes defined in `.cpp` files are listed as "no C++ class decl found".
- Non-polymorphic doors' individual caller sites are not tabled (counts only, T3).
- Ghidra: not used, so no retail-side re-check of the 135 rows' `(ddVa, dtorVa, size)` triples beyond what
  `tools/rt_deleting_dtor_verify.py` already guarantees (not re-run).

## How measured

Helper scripts in `C:/Users/USPC/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/b39/0B/` (Python 3.12 + `pefile`), all scanning
`runtime/ standalone/ engine/ code/ dx11/ tests/` (`.cpp .h .inc .hpp .c`) with `standalone/build*`,
`tests/unit/build`, `.claude/worktrees` excluded, comments stripped (`//` and `/* */`, strings kept):
- `common.py` — file walk, comment stripper, `rt_export_addrs.inc` parser (4,926 exports; 517 `??0`, 199 `??1`),
  the exe PE import table (149 Runtime ctor/dtor import slots), mangled-owner demangler.
- `doors.py` — every code line naming a `??0/??1` export by string literal, by its Runtime VA, or by an exe import
  slot that resolves to one (1,510 occurrences); classified registration / exe-slot registration / replay row / use.
- `bind.py` — resolves each use to call sites BY BINDING: immediate call, named constant, `engine_funcs` global by
  initialiser (followed into every file incl. `engine_funcs.h` inline bodies), file statics, locals (windowed), one
  level of local→global copy; DEAD = resolved and never called.
- `agg.py` — per registered name: door symbol(s), definition file:line, body traits (stamps, shared body, delegation),
  direct uses of the door symbol; `factory_ui1` table-driven registrations added from `factory_ui1_tables.inc`.
- `classes.py` — class/struct bodies in headers: ctor/dtor/`DeletingDtor` declarations.
- `deldtor.py`, `slot0.py`, `stampers.py` — the 135 rows, their wrappers and registrations, the minted slot each
  fills (all 152 tables' slot 0 classified), by-address uses, `RtDelDtor_` symbol uses, the stamper count per table.
- `partc.py`, `partc2.py` — the destruction-dispatch patterns of T6.
- `gen_md.py`, `emit.py` — verdict rules and these tables. Rule: EXT callers → "convert callers then delete";
  only Runtime non-door callers → "(Runtime-internal)"; only RTDELDTOR / FACTORY / DOORCHAIN → "dies with the mint";
  none → "no caller by binding"; only DEAD → "delete now"; non-polymorphic → "stays"; dx11 `*Data` ctors → "stays".
